Improve local method. Let's design a solid improvement:

Current algorithm:
- key = last up to 12 chars of prefix (including whitespace), require trimmed length >= 3.
- Scan corpus + learned; `rfind(key)`; tail = rest of line, trimmed end; count; pick highest count (ties shorter).
- Requires count >= 2, learned weight 3.
Limitations:
- Requires 2 occurrences, so in a fresh file with no repeats, no predictions.
- Uses only current buffer + other open buffers + learned lines; no project files not opened.
- Suffix can match inside identifiers or strings; e.g., "let " may suggest random tails.
- Predicts only at end of line.
- Doesn't prefer candidates that follow the same indentation or line prefix.

```rust

We'll implement two paths:
1. **Line completion** (cursor at end, prefix non-whitespace):
   - Candidate keys: last 16, 12, 8 chars of prefix (trim trailing spaces? Keep exact).

   - Score = match weight (learned 4, project 1.5? use integer counts: project/open 1, learned 3) + length bonus + indentation bonus + previous-line context bonus.
   - To scan efficiently and prefer candidates:
     - For each candidate line, use `rfind(key)`. Get tail.
     - Only consider if the tail starts with a plausible character? No.
     - Indentation bonus: compare leading whitespace of candidate line with current line's indentation. If equal, +2. If candidate indent is deeper and current line ends with '{' etc., +1.

We do iterate lines; we could use the previous corpus line as context. Build corpus as a slice of lines from buffers; we can iterate with index and compare `corpus[i-1]`'s trimmed suffix to the previous line. Bonus if the previous line's last 8 chars match. This is strong context.
   - Require count >= threshold: for learned candidates, count weight >= 3; for corpus, total count >= 2. Use a BTreeMap<tail, score>.
   - Choose the best score; tie shorter tail/longer key.
2. **Next-line prediction** (cursor line is blank or only indentation):
   - Key = previous line trimmed_end last 16 chars + "|" + indentation.
   - Use `transitions`: stored pairs (prev_key, next_line). Also derive transitions on the fly from corpus: for each i>0, if corpus[i-1] suffix matches prev key, consider corpus[i] as next line, requiring corpus[i] starts with the current indentation? We can add current indent to the key or filter candidate lines that start with the indent.

Simplify: implement both in one `suggest` that receives `corpus: &[&str]`, `line`, `col`, `previous_line`, and `indent`.
- If cursor at end and prefix has non-whitespace: line completion.
- If prefix is only whitespace (or empty) and previous_line exists: next-line prediction.

Scoring details:
- Use suffix keys of lengths 16/12/8/6, require trimmed length >= 3.
- For each corpus line and learned line, for the longest key found, compute tail.
- Weight: learned lines +3 per occurrence, corpus +1.
- Context bonus: if previous_line is Some and the corpus previous line ends with the same last 8 non-space chars as previous_line, +3.
- Indent bonus: candidate line's leading whitespace == current indentation → +2; if candidate tail starts with the same indent? Tail is after suffix; not relevant.
- Penalize candidate tail that starts with `//` or a quote? Maybe not.
- Return best if score >= 4? With one learned occurrence weight 3 + indent 2 = 5. Corpus occurrences 2 + indent 2 = 4. Single corpus occurrence + indent 2 = 3 < 4. Good.
- Candidate tail length <= 120, no control chars.
- If the candidate tail is only punctuation/brace and score low, still okay.

Next-line:
- Extract key from previous_line: last 20 chars trimmed_end.
- Scan corpus with window: for i in 1..len, if corpus[i-1].trim_end().ends_with(key), candidate = corpus[i]. If candidate starts with current indent, tail = candidate[indent.len()..]. If tail nonempty, score weight (learned? transitions) + indent match. Count duplicates. Require count >= 2 or learned transitions weight.
- Transitions learned: store `(String, String)` pairs where first = previous line trimmed_end suffix (last 40 chars), second = next line (full). When the current indent is blank, strip it from the candidate if it matches.
- Also learn transitions from buffers on save: for each adjacent pair, `learn_transition(prev, next)`. Cap 5000. Persist to `transitions.txt`.
- Return best tail.

Persistence:
- learned lines: existing file.
- transitions: new file in same cache dir, lines encoded as `prev\tnext` with tabs escaped? Lines can contain tabs. Use JSON? serde_json is already a dependency. Store as JSON array of [prev,next]? Simpler: separate files with tab delimiter; code lines may contain tabs but not in prev key (we use trimmed end, could contain tabs). Use JSON Lines: `{"p":"...","n":"..."}` via serde_json. But predict.rs currently doesn't import serde. serde_json is available. Use `serde_json::json!` and parse Value. Or use a simple escaping: replace `\` with `\\`, `\n` with `\n`, `\t` with `\t`; more code. Use JSON lines.
- On load, read transitions file, parse each line as Value with p/n.
- Persist on learn by appending a JSON line.
- Cap transitions in memory; disk can grow; load caps.

    pub fn files(&self) -> Vec<String> {
        let mut files = Vec::new();
        collect_files(&self.root, &self.root, &mut files);
        files.sort_by_key(|path| path.to_lowercase());
        files
    }

    pub fn reveal(&mut self, path: &Path) {
        let Ok(relative) = path.strip_prefix(&self.root) else {
            return;
        };

        let mut current = 0usize;
        let mut found = true;
        for component in relative.components() {
            let std::path::Component::Normal(name) = component else {
                continue;
            };
            let name = name.to_string_lossy();
            let children = self.nodes[current].children.clone();
            let Some(child) = children
                .into_iter()
                .find(|index| self.nodes[*index].name == name)
            else {
                found = false;
                break;
            };
            if self.nodes[child].is_dir {
                if !self.nodes[child].loaded {
                    self.load_children(child);
                }
                self.nodes[child].expanded = true;
            }
            current = child;
        }

        if !found {
            return;
        }

        self.rebuild_visible();
        if let Some(position) = self.visible.iter().position(|index| *index == current) {
            self.selected = position;
        }
    }

        let Ok(relative) = path.strip_prefix(&self.root) else {

const MAX_LEARNED: usize = 3000;
const MAX_TAIL: usize = 120;
const MAX_PROJECT_LINES: usize = 30_000;
const MAX_TRANSITIONS: usize = 4000;

pub struct Predictor {
    learned: Vec<String>,
    transitions: Vec<(String, String)>,
    project: Vec<String>,
    path: Option<PathBuf>,
    transition_path: Option<PathBuf>,
}

impl Predictor {
    pub fn load() -> Self { ... load both files ... }
    pub fn add_project_lines(&mut self, lines: Vec<String>) {
        if lines.is_empty() { return; }
        self.project.extend(lines);
        if self.project.len() > MAX_PROJECT_LINES {
            let start = self.project.len() - MAX_PROJECT_LINES;
            self.project.drain(0..start);
        }
    }
    pub fn suggest(&self, buffer_lines: &[String], other_lines: ???, line: &str, col: usize, previous: Option<&str>, indent: &str) -> Option<String>
```

We need the corpus. Editor can pass a `Vec<&str>` built from buffers + predictor project + learned? But suggest is a method on Predictor; it can add its own learned/project, while caller passes buffer corpus. Signature:
```rust
pub fn suggest<'a, I>(&self, corpus: I, line: &str, col: usize, previous: Option<&str>, indent: &str) -> Option<String>
where I: IntoIterator<Item=&'a str>
```
Then inside, iterate corpus + self.learned + self.project; transitions + derive from corpus/project.

Line completion:
- If prefix.trim().is_empty(): next-line path.
- Else:
  - keys: suffix lengths [16, 12, 8, 6] from prefix (trim trailing whitespace? At line end, prefix may end spaces; keep exact? A key ending in spaces is less useful. Use `prefix.trim_end()` for key, but then the ghost would be inserted at cursor after spaces, misaligned. For line completion, only predict when prefix does not end with whitespace? Most code typing ends with a non-space. If it ends with space, we can still use the trimmed key but the tail should account for the spaces; complex. Simpler: if prefix ends with whitespace beyond the leading indent, skip line completion (return maybe next-line? no). Actually typing `let ` ends with a space; predictions could be useful. But matching exact `let ` keys works: corpus lines contain `let `. Keep exact key including trailing spaces.
  - For each key length, for each corpus line + learned + project:
    - rfind(key); tail = after; trim_end; valid.
    - Compute candidate full line indentation = leading whitespace of the corpus line.
    - Score:
      - base = weight (learned 4, project 1, buffers 1)
      - key_len bonus: use key length (longer better) * 2? We iterate keys; use longest key that produces candidates? Could aggregate score with key_len.
      - indent match: if candidate_indent == indent, +2; else if candidate_indent.starts_with(indent) && current line ends with block opener, +1; else if current indent empty, +0.
      - previous-line context: if previous is Some and candidate line's preceding corpus line trim_end ends with previous_trim_suffix (last 12 chars), +3.
    - Store BTreeMap<tail, (score, count)>. Aggregate counts.
  - Require total score >= 4 (with learned weight 4, qualifies; two buffer occurrences 2, plus indent 2 = 4; one occurrence + indent 2 + prev context 3 = 6).
  - Choose best by score then shorter tail? Prefer higher score; tie longer key or shorter tail.
  - Avoid returning a tail that is just the current line's indentation or repeated punctuation. 
  - Max tail 120 chars.
- Next-line:
  - previous line required; key = last 20 chars of previous.trim_end(); if key len < 3 return None.
  - Consider `self.transitions` (weight 4) and adjacent corpus/project lines (weight 1, +2 if prev context exactly matches).
  - Candidate next line: if it starts with `indent`, tail = next[indent.len()..]; if tail empty skip. If it doesn't start with indent but indent is empty, tail = full line.
  - Avoid suggesting a line identical to the previous line? Not necessarily.
  - Require score >= 4.
  - Return the tail (without indent) to insert after existing indent.

130:     rx: Receiver<LlmResult>,
131:     next_id: u64,
132: }
133: 
134: struct LlmRequest {
135:     id: u64,
136:     pi: PathBuf,
137:     root: PathBuf,
138:     model: Option<String>,
139:     prompt: String,
140: }
141: 
142: impl LlmPredictor {
143:     pub fn new() -> Self {
144:         let (tx, requests) = mpsc::channel::<LlmRequest>();
145:         let (responses, rx) = mpsc::channel::<LlmResult>();
146:         thread::spawn(move || {
147:             while let Ok(request) = requests.recv() {
148:                 let result = run_pi_completion(&request);
149:                 if responses.send((request.id, result)).is_err() {
150:                     break;
151:                 }
152:             }
153:         });
154:         Self { tx, rx, next_id: 0 }
155:     }
156: 
157:     pub fn request(
158:         &mut self,
159:         pi: &Path,
160:         root: &Path,
161:         model: Option<&str>,
162:         prompt: String,
163:     ) -> Option<u64> {
164:         self.next_id = self.next_id.wrapping_add(1);
165:         let id = self.next_id;
166:         let request = LlmRequest {
167:             id,
168:             pi: pi.to_path_buf(),
169:             root: root.to_path_buf(),
170:             model: model.map(str::to_string),
171:             prompt,
172:         };
173:         self.tx.send(request).ok()?;
174:         Some(id)
175:     }
176: 
177:     pub fn poll(&self) -> Option<LlmResult> {
178:         self.rx.try_recv().ok()
179:     }
180: }
181: 
182: fn completion_args(model: Option<&str>, prompt: &str) -> Vec<String> {
183:     let mut args = [
184:         "--print",
185:         "--no-session",
186:         "--no-tools",
187:         "--no-context-files",
188:         "--no-skills",
189:         "--no-prompt-templates",
190:         "--no-mcp",
191:         "--no-approve",
192:         "--thinking",
193:         "off",
194:         "--system-prompt",
195:         LLM_SYSTEM,
196:     ]
197:     .iter()
198:     .map(|arg| (*arg).to_string())
199:     .collect::<Vec<_>>();
200:     if let Some(model) = model.filter(|model| !model.is_empty()) {
201:         args.push("--model".to_string());
202:         args.push(model.to_string());
203:     }
204:     args.push(prompt.to_string());
205:     args
206: }
207: 
208: fn run_pi_completion(request: &LlmRequest) -> Result<String, String> {
209:     let output = Command::new(&request.pi)
210:         .current_dir(&request.root)
211:         .args(completion_args(request.model.as_deref(), &request.prompt))
212:         .stdin(Stdio::null())
213:         .output()
214:         .map_err(|error| format!("prediction: {error}"))?;
215:     if !output.status.success() {
216:         let error = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);
217:         let error = error.lines().last().unwrap_or("pi failed");
218:         return Err(format!("prediction: {error}"));
219:     }
220:     Ok(String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout).to_string())
221: }
222: 
223: fn config_path() -> Option<PathBuf> {
224:     if let Some(base) = env::var_os("XDG_CONFIG_HOME") {
225:         return Some(PathBuf::from(base).join("smol").join("predictions.json"));
226:     }
227:     env::var_os("HOME").map(|home| {
228:         PathBuf::from(home)
229:             .join(".config")
230:             .join("smol")
231:             .join("predictions.json")
232:     })
233: }
234: 
235: pub struct Predictor {
236:     learned: Vec<String>,
237:     path: Option<PathBuf>,
238: }
239: 
240: impl Predictor {
241:     pub fn load() -> Self {
242:         let path = cache_path();
243:         let learned = path
244:             .as_ref()
245:             .and_then(|path| fs::read_to_string(path).ok())
246:             .map(|text| {
247:                 text.lines()
248:                     .filter(|line| !line.trim().is_empty())
249:                     .map(|line| line.trim_end().to_string())
250:                     .collect::<Vec<_>>()
251:             })
252:             .unwrap_or_default();
253: 
254:         let mut predictor = Self { learned, path };
255:         if predictor.learned.len() > MAX_LEARNED {
256:             let start = predictor.learned.len() - MAX_LEARNED;
257:             predictor.learned = predictor.learned.split_off(start);
258:         }
259:         predictor
260:     }
261: 
262:     pub fn suggest<'a, I>(&self, corpus: I, line: &str, col: usize) -> Option<String>
263:     where
264:         I: IntoIterator<Item = &'a str>,
265:     {
266:         let prefix: String = line.chars().take(col).collect();
267:         let key: String = prefix
268:             .chars()
269:             .rev()
270:             .take(12)
271:             .collect::<Vec<_>>()
272:             .into_iter()
273:             .rev()
274:             .collect();
275: 
276:         if key.trim().chars().count() < 3 {
277:             return None;
278:         }
279: 
280:         let mut counts: BTreeMap<String, u32> = BTreeMap::new();
281: 
282:         for candidate in corpus {
283:             add_candidate(&mut counts, candidate, &key, 1);
284:         }
285:         for candidate in &self.learned {
286:             add_candidate(&mut counts, candidate, &key, 3);
287:         }
288: 
289:         let mut best: Option<(u32, String)> = None;
290:         for (tail, count) in counts {
291:             if count < 2 {
292:                 continue;
293:             }
294:             let replace = match &best {
295:                 None => true,
296:                 Some((best_count, best_tail)) => {
297:                     count > *best_count || (count == *best_count && tail.len() < best_tail.len())
298:                 }
299:             };
300:             if replace {
301:                 best = Some((count, tail));
302:             }
303:         }
304: 
305:         best.map(|(_, tail)| tail)
306:     }
307: 
308:     pub fn learn(&mut self, line: &str) {
309:         let line = line.trim_end();
310:         if line.trim().is_empty() || line.chars().count() > 400 {
311:             return;
312:         }
313: 
314:         self.learned.retain(|known| known != line);
315:         self.learned.push(line.to_string());
316:         if self.learned.len() > MAX_LEARNED {
317:             self.learned.remove(0);
318:         }
319: 
320:         let Some(path) = &self.path else {
321:             return;
322:         };
323:         if let Some(parent) = path.parent() {
324:             let _ = fs::create_dir_all(parent);
325:         }
326:         if let Ok(mut file) = OpenOptions::new().create(true).append(true).open(path) {
327:             let _ = writeln!(file, "{line}");
328:         }
329:     }
330: }
331: 
332: fn add_candidate(counts: &mut BTreeMap<String, u32>, text: &str, key: &str, weight: u32) {
333:     let Some(pos) = text.rfind(key) else {
334:         return;
335:     };
336:     let tail = text[pos + key.len()..].trim_end();
337:     if tail.is_empty() || tail.chars().count() > MAX_TAIL {
338:         return;
339:     }
340:     if tail.chars().any(char::is_control) {
341:         return;
342:     }
343:     *counts.entry(tail.to_string()).or_insert(0) += weight;
344: }
345: 
346: fn cache_path() -> Option<PathBuf> {
347:     if let Some(base) = env::var_os("XDG_CACHE_HOME") {
348:         return Some(PathBuf::from(base).join("smol").join("predictions.txt"));
349:     }
350:     env::var_os("HOME").map(|home| {
351:         PathBuf::from(home)
352:             .join(".cache")
353:             .join("smol")
354:             .join("predictions.txt")
355:     })
356: }

Thinking
Now write the new predict.rs. Let's craft carefully.

Design details and code:

```rust
use std::collections::BTreeMap;
use std::env;
use std::fs::{self, OpenOptions};
use std::io::Write;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};

const MAX_LEARNED: usize = 3000;
const MAX_TRANSITIONS: usize = 4000;
const MAX_PROJECT_LINES: usize = 30_000;
const MAX_TAIL: usize = 120;
const MAX_KEY: usize = 20;

pub struct Predictor {
    learned: Vec<String>,
    transitions: Vec<(String, String)>,
    project: Vec<String>,
    path: Option<PathBuf>,
    transitions_path: Option<PathBuf>,
}
```

`load()`:
- cache_file(name) helper.
- read learned lines.
- read transitions: each line JSON `{"p":...,"n":...}` using serde_json::Value. Need `serde_json` import. Or avoid serde and use tab with escaping. We already have serde_json dependency (used elsewhere in app). Use it.
- Cap.

`add_project_lines`:
```rust
pub fn add_project_lines(&mut self, lines: Vec<String>) {
    if lines.is_empty() { return; }
    self.project.extend(lines.into_iter().filter(|line| line.chars().count() <= 400));
    if self.project.len() > MAX_PROJECT_LINES {
        let start = self.project.len() - MAX_PROJECT_LINES;
        self.project.drain(0..start);
    }
}
```

`learn` as before.

`learn_lines(&mut self, lines: &[String])`:
```rust
for pair in lines.windows(2) {
   self.learn_transition(&pair[0], &pair[1]);
}
```
`learn_transition`:
```rust
fn learn_transition(&mut self, prev: &str, next: &str) {
    let key = transition_key(prev);
    if key.is_empty() || next.trim().is_empty() || next.chars().count() > 400 { return; }
    let next = next.trim_end();
    if let Some(pos) = self.transitions.iter().position(|(p,n)| p == &key && n == next) {
        let entry = self.transitions.remove(pos);
        self.transitions.push(entry);
    } else {
        self.transitions.push((key.clone(), next.to_string()));
        if self.transitions.len() > MAX_TRANSITIONS { self.transitions.remove(0); }
    }
    // persist JSON line
    if let Some(path)=&self.transitions_path { ... writeln!(file, "{}", json!({"p":key,"n":next})) }
}
```
Dedupe O(n) per pair on save with 4k transitions and a file with hundreds of lines → could be 4k*500 = 2M comparisons per save, okay but maybe slow. Use a HashSet? Simplicity: only learn transitions on save if the buffer has <2000 lines; dedupe with a BTreeSet? We can maintain transitions as Vec and check `.iter().position`; for 4k it's fine (2M string comparisons ~ few ms). Acceptable.

`transition_key(prev)`: last MAX_KEY chars of prev.trim_end(); require trimmed len >= 3.
Note: Indentation in prev is included in the key; good.

`suggest` signature:
```rust
pub fn suggest(
    &self,
    corpus: &[&str],
    line: &str,
    col: usize,
    previous: Option<&str>,
    indent: &str,
) -> Option<String>
```
- prefix = chars[..col] (caller ensures col <= len).
- if prefix.trim().is_empty() → `self.suggest_next(corpus, previous, indent)`.
- else `self.suggest_line(corpus, prefix, previous, indent)`.

`suggest_line`:
```rust
let keys: Vec<String> = key_lengths(&prefix); // [16,12,8,6] suffixes, dedupe, only if trimmed len>=3
let mut scores: BTreeMap<String, Score> = BTreeMap::new();
for (key_len, key) in keys.iter() {
   for candidate in corpus { self.score_line(candidate, key, key_len, previous, indent, 1, &mut scores); }
   for candidate in &self.learned { self.score_line(candidate, key, key_len, previous, indent, 4, &mut scores); }
   for candidate in &self.project { ... weight 1 ... }
}
best score >= MIN_SCORE (4)
```
But `score_line` needs the candidate's previous line for context. For corpus, we have adjacent lines; for self.project, also adjacent; for learned, no adjacent context. Pass `prev_context: Option<&str>`.
- For corpus (slice), iterate with index: `let prev = i.checked_sub(1).and_then(|j| corpus.get(j)).copied();`.
- For self.project, `self.project[i-1]`.
- For learned, None.

Collect corpus refs: `corpus` already includes active + other buffers. Should we also include project lines in the corpus for `suggest_next` transitions. Yes, iterate project adjacent.

Score struct:
```rust
struct Score { score: u32, count: u32 }
```
Store in BTreeMap<tail, (u32,u32)>.

score_line:
```rust
fn score_line(&self, candidate: &str, key: &str, key_len: usize, prev: Option<&str>, indent: &str, weight: u32, out: &mut BTreeMap<String,(u32,u32)>) {
    let Some(pos) = candidate.rfind(key) else { return; };
    let tail = candidate[pos+key.len()..].trim_end();
    if tail.is_empty() || tail.chars().count() > MAX_TAIL || tail.chars().any(char::is_control) { return; }
    // Avoid suggesting text that starts with a closing delimiter when the current line has an opener? no.
    let mut score = weight * 2 + (key_len as u32);
    let candidate_indent = leading_whitespace(candidate);
    if candidate_indent == indent { score += 4; }
    else if indent.is_empty() || candidate_indent.starts_with(indent) { score += 1; }
    if let (Some(prev), Some(prev_candidate)) = (prev, prev_context) { ... } // need pass prev candidate line
    ...
    out.entry(tail.to_string()).and_modify(|(s,c)| { *s += score; *c += 1 }).or_insert((score,1));
}
```
To compare previous-line context, pass `prev_candidate: Option<&str>` (the candidate's preceding line in the corpus). Compute:
```rust
if let (Some(prev), Some(candidate_prev)) = (prev, prev_candidate) {
    let prev_key = tail_key(prev, 12);
    if !prev_key.is_empty() && candidate_prev.trim_end().ends_with(&prev_key) { score += 4; }
}
```
`tail_key(prev, n)` last n chars trimmed_end, require >=3.

But `prev` is the actual previous line in the editor (full line). Its suffix may include indentation. Good.

Scoring/min threshold: weight 1 * 2 + key_len (6..16) = 8..18; indent match +4; prev context +4. A single corpus candidate with a 6-char key, no indent/context = 8, which would pass MIN_SCORE 6 and produce noise from one occurrence. Require count >= 2 OR weight >= 4 (learned). We track count (occurrences) and score. Selection rule:
- Candidate qualifies if `count >= 2 || weight learned contributed`. We don't track weight type in aggregate. Simpler: store `(score, count, learned_hit)`; or require `count >= 2 || score >= 12` (a single learned: weight 4*2 + key 8 = 16; qualifies; a single corpus with prev+indent: 8+4+4=16 → qualifies, which might be okay if strong context). Set threshold: `count >= 2 || score >= 14`. A single project/corpus with strong context qualifies. That's reasonable for local completions.
- Also require total score >= 8.
- Current learned weight 4; a learned line key match: 8 + key 8 = 16 → passes.
- Tie-break: higher score, then shorter tail? Prefer longer tail? For usefulness, prefer higher count/score; for ties, shorter tail.

But with multiple key lengths, score adds up, so a candidate matching both 16-char and 12-char keys gets double score. We should only score each candidate line once with its **longest matching key**. Iterate keys longest-first; for each candidate, if it matches a key, score and break. But we also want to prefer longer keys globally. Implement:
```rust
for candidate in all lines with context {
  let mut best_match: Option<(key_len, pos)> = None;
  for key in keys { if let Some(pos)=candidate.rfind(key) { best_match=Some((key.len(),pos)); break; } }
  ...
}
```
This avoids double counting. Keys are unicode char lengths; use `key.chars().count()` for score. Actually byte length is fine for ordering; use chars.

We need to distinguish learned lines (higher weight) and corpus/project (weight 1). Implement helper `consider(&self, candidate, prev_candidate, weight, ...)`.

All candidate lines:
```rust
fn all_candidates<'a>(&'a self, corpus: &'a [&'a str]) -> Vec<(String, Option<&'a str>, u32)>? 
```
Borrow/lifetime complexity. Instead, run the scoring loop three times:
1. corpus slice with adjacent prev and weight 1.
2. project slice with adjacent prev and weight 1.
3. learned with prev None and weight 4.

`score_candidate` takes `candidate: &str, prev_candidate: Option<&str>, keys: &[String], prev: Option<&str>, indent: &str, weight: u32, scores: &mut ...`.

Key list: suffixes of prefix at lengths [16,12,8,6], but only if the trimmed key length >= 3. Also include the full prefix if shorter? If prefix length < 6, use it; e.g., `let ` (4 chars) should match. Current min 3. Add lengths [16,12,8,6,4] and dedupe by actual string; if prefix shorter, the suffix is the whole prefix. Ensure key.trim().chars().count() >= 3.
- For trailing-space prefixes, key includes spaces; fine.
- We might also want to try a key that is the whole **current line prefix trimmed_end**? The last 16 covers.

`leading_whitespace(candidate)`: chars take_while ' ' or '\t'.

Indent comparison: use the candidate line's indentation. `indent` is the current line's leading whitespace (which may be only whitespace if next-line). For line completion, the current line prefix includes indent; candidate indentation match is a good signal.

Previous-line context suffix length: use 12 chars. If the editor previous line has only 2 chars, skip.

`tail_key(prev, n)` must trim_end then take last n chars and require >=3 after trim.

Next-line:
```rust
fn suggest_next(&self, corpus: &[&str], previous: Option<&str>, indent: &str) -> Option<String> {
    let prev = previous?;
    let key = tail_key(prev, MAX_KEY)?;
    let mut scores: BTreeMap<String,(u32,u32)> = BTreeMap::new();
    // explicit learned transitions
    for (p,n) in &self.transitions {
        if p == &key { add_next(n, indent, 4, &mut scores); }
        else if p.ends_with(&key) { add_next(n, indent, 2, &mut scores); } // key is a suffix; p may include more context
    }
    // corpus transitions
    for i in 1..corpus.len() {
        if let Some(k) = tail_key(corpus[i-1], MAX_KEY) { if k == key { add_next(corpus[i], indent, 1, &mut scores); } }
    }
    for i in 1..self.project.len() {
        if tail_key(&self.project[i-1], MAX_KEY).as_deref() == Some(key.as_str()) { add_next(&self.project[i], indent, 1, &mut scores); }
    }
    // choose best with count>=2 or score >= 6
}
```
`add_next(next_line, indent, weight, scores)`:
- let next = next_line.trim_end();
- if next.trim().is_empty() or len>400 or contains control → skip.
- let next_indent = leading_whitespace(next);
- Tail:
  - If indent.is_empty(): tail = next (but for a blank line at top-level, insert full next line).
  - Else if next_indent.starts_with(indent): tail = next[indent.len()..] (char-based; use chars).
  - Else if next_indent == indent: ...
  - Else: skip? If the candidate is at a shallower indent (e.g., closing brace), predicting it after our indent might be wrong. Allow if next_indent.len() < indent.len() and indent.starts_with(next_indent) → tail = next[next_indent.len()..]? That would include less indent than the user typed; duplicate. Skip shallower candidates for now.
- Candidate tail may be empty if next == indent; skip.
- Score: weight*2 + indent_bonus (next_indent == indent → 4; else 0).
- Use BTreeMap<tail,(score,count)>.
- Require count >= 2 || score >= 6; choose best.
- For learned transitions, a single occurrence weight 4*2=8 + indent 4 = 12 → qualifies.
- Corpus next line requires two occurrences (2+2=4? with indent 4 → 8) or one with indent 4 + weight 2 = 6 → qualifies; that may be fine (strong indent match).
- Avoid returning a tail equal to the current indent content? The cursor line is only indent; tail is after indent. Good.

Wait: if the user is on an empty line with indent "", and previous line ends with "{", a candidate next line "    let x = 1;" has next_indent "    " which doesn't start with "" — actually every string starts with ""; `starts_with("")` is true. tail = next["".len()..] = entire line including indentation. Good. Inserting the full line after an empty current line works.

If indent is "    " and candidate next_indent is "    ", tail = rest. Good.
If candidate next_indent is "        " (deeper), tail includes the extra 4 spaces + content; that may be desired for nested block. Allow if starts_with(indent).
If candidate is a closing brace at indent "" while user indent is "    ", skip (shallower).

Learning transitions on accept:
- In `accept_prediction`, after inserting ghost:
  - full_line = current line after insertion (for line completion) or current indent+ghost (for next-line).
  - `predictor.learn(&full_line)`.
  - `predictor.learn_transition(previous_line, &full_line)` for next-line; for line completion, also transition from previous line to full line? Yes, it can help. Get previous line before insertion.
- Editor has access to buffer lines; implement.

Learn from saved buffers:
- `Editor::save_active`: after fs::write, get lines clone and call `self.predictor.learn_lines(&lines);`. This may block briefly; okay.
- Maybe also learn transitions when files are opened (for other files)? The project scan covers transitions on the fly; no need.

Project scan:
- Implement `pub fn scan_project(root: &Path) -> Vec<String>` in predict.rs.
- Use `walk(root, depth, &mut files, &mut bytes, &mut lines)`.
- Skip ignored names: ".git", "target", "node_modules", ".freebuff", ".smol", ".cache", "dist", "build"? Keep the same as tree: .git, node_modules, target, .freebuff, .smol. Also skip hidden dirs? `.github` contains yml useful; don't skip all hidden. Skip the ignored list.
- Max files 300, bytes 4MB, lines 20k, depth 8, per-file 256KB.
- Read bytes, skip NUL, invalid UTF8, files with huge lines.
- Sort? Deterministic: collect file paths sorted, then read. Use `read_dir` collect and sort by name.
- Return lines.
- Tests: create a temp project with a few files, assert scan lines and prediction uses project corpus. Also test transition next-line, accent suffix.

Need to ensure the background scan in App doesn't run during tests. Use `cfg!(test)`.

Now Editor compute_ghost:
```rust
fn compute_ghost(&self) -> Option<String> {
    let buffer = self.buffers.get(self.active)?;
    if buffer.selection.is_some() { return None; }
    let line = buffer.lines.get(buffer.cursor_line)?;
    if buffer.cursor_col != char_count(line) { return None; }
    if buffer.lines.len() > 60_000 { return None; }
    let indent: String = line.chars().take_while(|c| *c==' '||*c=='\t').collect();
    let previous = buffer.cursor_line.checked_sub(1).and_then(|i| buffer.lines.get(i)).map(String::as_str);
    let mut corpus: Vec<&str> = buffer.lines.iter().map(String::as_str).collect();
    for (i, other) in self.buffers.iter().enumerate() { if i==self.active {continue;} for l in &other.lines { corpus.push(l); } }
    self.predictor.suggest(&corpus, line, buffer.cursor_col, previous, &indent)
}
```

1346: fn sanitize_llm(raw: &str, max_lines: usize) -> Option<String> {
1347:     let mut text = raw.replace("\r\n", "\n").replace('\r', "\n");
1348:     text = text.trim_matches('\n').to_string();
1349:     if text.starts_with("```") {
1350:         text = text.lines().skip(1).collect::<Vec<_>>().join("\n");
1351:         if text.trim_end().ends_with("```") {
1352:             text.truncate(text.trim_end().len() - 3);
1353:         }
1354:     }
1355:     let mut out = String::new();
1356:     for (index, line) in text.lines().take(max_lines.max(1)).enumerate() {
1357:         if index > 0 {
1358:             out.push('\n');
1359:         }
1360:         out.push_str(line.trim_end());
1361:     }
1362:     let out: String = out.chars().take(500).collect();
1363:     if out.trim().is_empty() {
1364:         None
1365:     } else {
1366:         Some(out)
1367:     }
1368: }

1373:         return text.to_string();
1374:     }
1375:     text.chars().skip(count - limit).collect()
1376: }

1380:         return text.to_string();

1382:     text.chars().take(limit).collect()
1383: }

2009:         if let MenuAction::OpenMenu(kind) = action {
2010:             let page = self.menu_page(kind);
2011:             if let Some(menu) = self.menu.as_mut() {

2013:             }
2014:             return;
2015:         }

2017:             action,
2018:             MenuAction::ToggleWrap
2019:                 | MenuAction::ToggleAgent
2020:                 | MenuAction::ToggleGit

2022:                 | MenuAction::CyclePredictionLines
2023:                 | MenuAction::CyclePredictionContext
2024:         );

2026:             self.menu = None;
2027:         }

2030:             MenuAction::ToggleWrap => {
2031:                 let wrap = self.editor.toggle_wrap();

2033:                     "word wrap on"
2034:                 } else {
2035:                     "word wrap off"
2036:                 });
2037:             }

2039:             MenuAction::ToggleGit => {

2046:                 };

1. MenuAction variants removal.

Imports: add `std::sync::mpsc::{self, Receiver, TryRecvError};` and `std::thread;`. Check current imports at top of app.rs. We saw HashMap, fs, Path, PathBuf, Duration, Instant, crossterm..., Rect, serde_json, crate. Add.

For the scan thread: `crate::predict::scan_project`. Use `sender`.

App::new:
```rust
let project_lines = if cfg!(test) {
    None
} else {
    let (tx, rx) = mpsc::channel();
    let scan_root = root.clone();
    thread::spawn(move || { let lines = crate::predict::scan_project(&scan_root); let _ = tx.send(lines); });
    Some(rx)
};
```
But `root` is moved into Self later; clone before. Place before `let mut app = Self { root, ... }`.

Tick:
```rust
if let Some(rx) = &self.project_lines {
    match rx.try_recv() {
        Ok(lines) => { self.editor.add_project_lines(lines); self.project_lines = None; }
        Err(TryRecvError::Empty) => {}
        Err(TryRecvError::Disconnected) => self.project_lines = None,
    }
}
```
Borrow: rx immutably borrowed from self.project_lines while mutating self.editor and self.project_lines. Cannot assign self.project_lines = None while borrow active. Use a local enum:
```rust
let received = self.project_lines.as_ref().and_then(|rx| match rx.try_recv() {
    Ok(lines) => Some(Ok(lines)),
    Err(TryRecvError::Disconnected) => Some(Err(())),
    Err(TryRecvError::Empty) => None,
});
match received {
  Some(Ok(lines)) => { self.project_lines = None; self.editor.add_project_lines(lines); }
  Some(Err(())) => { self.project_lines = None; }
  None => {}
}
```
Simpler:
```rust
let mut project_lines = None;
let mut disconnected = false;
if let Some(rx) = &self.project_lines {
    match rx.try_recv() { Ok(lines)=>project_lines=Some(lines), Err(TryRecvError::Empty)=>{}, Err(TryRecvError::Disconnected)=>disconnected=true }
}
if disconnected { self.project_lines = None; }
if let Some(lines)=project_lines { self.project_lines=None; self.editor.add_project_lines(lines); }
```

Apply edits.

Next-line prediction fails. Debug logic:

Test: previous = "    if ready {", indent = "    ". suggest(&[], "    ", 4, Some(prev), "    "). In suggest: prefix = "    " (line chars take 4) — trim empty → suggest_next.
key = tail_key(previous, MAX_KEY=20) = last 20 chars of "    if ready {" (14 chars) = entire "    if ready {".
transitions: learn_transition("    if ready {", "        run();"):
- key = "    if ready {".
- next = "        run();".
- guard: `if !next.starts_with(leading_whitespace(previous)) && !leading_whitespace(previous).is_empty() { return; }` — leading_whitespace(previous) = "    "; next starts with "    " → true, so !true = false → no return. Good.
- Push (key, next).
suggest_next: for known == key → add_next(scores, "        run();", indent="    ", weight 4).
add_next:
- next_indent = "        "; starts_with("    ") true.
- tail = next.chars().skip(indent.chars().count()).collect() → skip 4 chars → "    run();" (4 remaining spaces + run). Wait next has 8 spaces; skip 4 → 4 spaces + "run();". Expected tail "    run();" yes.
- score = 8; next_indent == indent? "        " != "    " → no +4. score 8.
pick_best: score 8 >= MIN_SCORE 8; count 1 and score < 12 → fails `(count < 2 && score < 12)` → skipped. So no result.

Fix scoring/threshold: a learned transition should qualify with one occurrence. Weight 4 → base 8; add indent match bonus for next_indent being deeper? The next line is nested one level deeper; that's expected after an `if {`. We should give a bonus when next_indent starts with indent and is deeper. Currently we only add 4 if equal; else no bonus. Add +4 when next_indent.starts_with(indent) and next_indent.len() > indent.len() (deeper), or simply +2. For the project pairs case, two occurrences count=2 with score 8 → passes score>=8 and count>=2. But test failed too? project pairs: add_next called twice, each score = weight*2=2 (weight 1) + maybe indent bonus. count 2, score 4 → MIN_SCORE 8 fails. So corpus next-line weight is too low.

Set add_next score:
- base = weight * 3 (learned 12, corpus 3).
- +4 if next_indent == indent.
- +2 if next_indent starts_with indent (deeper).
For learned: 12+2=14 → single qualifies (>=12).
For corpus: two occurrences 6 + 4 (deeper bonus each? 2 each = 4) = 10; count 2, score 10 → MIN_SCORE 8 passes. If same indent, 3+4=7 each → 14 for 2. Good.
For a single corpus with same indent: 3+4=7 <8; maybe too strict but okay. A single corpus deeper: 3+2=5. Fine.

Also `pick_best` condition `(count < 2 && score < 12)` means learned 14 qualifies; corpus count 2 passes regardless score as long as >=8.

Apply: in add_next:
```rust
let mut score = weight * 3;
if next_indent == indent { score += 4; } else if next_indent.starts_with(indent) { score += 2; }
```
Note next_indent.starts_with(indent) is checked earlier; if equal, first branch. Good.

Also `learn_transition` guard weird: it skips if next doesn't start with previous indentation. For a closing brace at shallower indent, it skips learning, which is fine. But previous indent empty → condition `!next.starts_with("")` false, so always allowed. Fine. However, the condition as written:
```rust
if !next.starts_with(leading_whitespace(previous)) && !leading_whitespace(previous).is_empty() { return; }
```
For previous indent "    " and next indent "        " → allowed. For previous indent "    " and next indent "" (closing brace) → skipped. Maybe we want to learn dedents too, but not needed.

Fix add_next and rerun tests.

The next-line path needs stronger scoring for learned/deeper-indented transitions. I'll adjust it.

94	Inline predictions are local and instant. They learn from open files, a bounded project-wide corpus, saved line transitions, and accepted predictions. Learned lines and transitions are stored in `~/.cache/smol/`. No network request, model download, or pi subprocess is involved.

But the test output `Some("e\");")` means the chosen tail was e");. Maybe the real line was matched with a **longer key** but scored lower? Let's compute: real line `    pub fn files(&self) -> Vec<String> {`; rfind for the 14-char key `    pub fn fil` → tail `es(&self) -> Vec<String> {`. Score:
- weight 1*2 = 2
- key chars 14
- candidate_indent = "    " == indent "    " → +4
= 20.
But wait, `keys` order is [16,12,8,6,4]; `find_map` picks the **first** key found. For the long key 16, the suffix string is the whole prefix (14 chars) because take(16) returns 14. For the 12 key, `  pub fn fil`; for 8 key, `ub fn fil`; etc. On the real line, the 16 key matches, tail as expected. Good.

The test line `let ghost = ... "    pub fn fil", 14...` also matches the 16 key, with tail `", 14, None, "    ");`, score: indent of line is 8 spaces? The line has 8 spaces (inside function inside mod). Current indent 4 → candidate_indent "        " starts_with "    " → +1. Score = 2 + 14 + 1 = 17. Lower than 20. So real wins.

Unless the *previous-line context* gives the test line +4 and something else... The test line's previous corpus line is `        let mut predictor = predictor();` or similar; previous key from editor previous = None in this test, so no bonus. Real line's prev none too. So real wins.

Why e");? Maybe the `scan_project` corpus includes the **test failure output**? No.

Wait: `pick_best` aggregates by tail. The real tail appears once, score 20. `e");` appears in multiple lines, accumulating score: line main.rs:179 tail e"); score 7, line main.rs:185? `file.expect("file path")` contains ` fil` → tail `e path")`; line 196, etc. Multiple `e");` occurrences each add score; could accumulate to >20. Specifically, many lines in main.rs containing ` fil` and ending `e");`:
- `let (root, file) = resolve_target(path).expect("resolve file");` → tail `e");` (score 7)
- `let (root, file) = resolve_target(path).expect("resolve folder");` → tail `e folder");` not e");
- `let mut file = ...` etc.
- Test lines `fs::write(&path, "hello\n").expect("write test file");` → contains ` fil` in "test file" and ends `e");` → tail `e");` score: indent 8? line has 8 spaces, current indent 4 → +1, key len 4 → 7. There are many such lines (10+), accumulating 7 each → >20. So aggregation over duplicate tails across lines lets noise win. This is a flaw in the scoring: candidates with the same tail from many unrelated lines accumulate score. We need to avoid short-key noise and/or score by best occurrence rather than sum.

Improvements:
1. Require the match to start at a word boundary or after whitespace for short keys? ` fil` matches inside "file"; the key starts with a space, so it's at a word boundary (` fil` in "test file"). Hard.
2. Prefer longer keys strongly: score key length exponentially, or only use the longest key that yields any candidate. Simple approach: iterate keys from longest to shortest; for the first key length that produces at least one candidate with score >= threshold, use only that key's candidates and stop. For the real prefix, the 14-char key matches real code (and test lines); candidates include real tail and test tail; choose real (higher indent bonus). Short keys won't be considered. This eliminates noise.
   - Implement: for each key in keys (longest first), collect scores; if any candidate qualifies (pick_best returns Some), return it. But pick_best currently could be polluted by test-line noise within the same key length (only lines containing the full 14-char key: real + 2 test lines). Real has higher score, so fine.
   - For prefixes shorter than 14, the 16-key is the whole prefix; good.
   - This is a strong improvement: longest-match-first.
3. Limit aggregation: use max score per tail instead of sum? Aggregation across occurrences is useful for repeated patterns (e.g., same line repeated). Keep sum but with longest-key gating.

Implement in `suggest_line`:
```rust
for key in &keys {
    let mut scores = BTreeMap::new();
    // score candidates with only this key (not all keys)
    ...
    if let Some(tail) = pick_best(scores) { return Some(tail); }
}
```
But `score_candidate` currently takes keys and finds the first; change to take a single key and match it. Then loop keys. This also simplifies. For each key level, if any qualifying candidate, return the best. Longest-first ensures context.

1: use std::collections::BTreeMap;
2: use std::env;
3: use std::fs::{self, OpenOptions};
4: use std::io::Write;
5: use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
6: 
7: use serde_json::{json, Value};
8: 
9: const MAX_LEARNED: usize = 3000;
10: const MAX_TRANSITIONS: usize = 4000;
11: const MAX_PROJECT_LINES: usize = 30_000;
12: const MAX_TAIL: usize = 120;
13: const MAX_LINE: usize = 400;
14: const MAX_KEY: usize = 20;
15: const MIN_SCORE: u32 = 8;
16: 
17: const IGNORED: &[&str] = &[".git", "node_modules", "target", ".freebuff", ".smol"];
18: 
19: pub struct Predictor {
20:     learned: Vec<String>,
21:     transitions: Vec<(String, String)>,
22:     project: Vec<String>,
23:     path: Option<PathBuf>,
24:     transitions_path: Option<PathBuf>,
25: }
26: 
27: impl Predictor {
28:     pub fn load() -> Self {
29:         let path = cache_path("predictions.txt");
30:         let learned = path
31:             .as_ref()
32:             .and_then(|path| fs::read_to_string(path).ok())
33:             .map(|text| {
34:                 text.lines()
35:                     .filter(|line| !line.trim().is_empty())
36:                     .map(|line| line.trim_end().to_string())
37:                     .collect::<Vec<_>>()
38:             })
39:             .unwrap_or_default();
40: 
41:         let transitions_path = cache_path("transitions.txt");
42:         let transitions = transitions_path
43:             .as_ref()
44:             .and_then(|path| fs::read_to_string(path).ok())
45:             .map(|text| {
46:                 text.lines()
47:                     .filter_map(|line| {
48:                         let value = serde_json::from_str::<Value>(line).ok()?;
49:                         let previous = value.get("p")?.as_str()?.to_string();
50:                         let next = value.get("n")?.as_str()?.to_string();
51:                         Some((previous, next))
52:                     })
53:                     .collect::<Vec<_>>()
54:             })
55:             .unwrap_or_default();
56: 
57:         let mut predictor = Self {
58:             learned,
59:             transitions,
60:             project: Vec::new(),
61:             path,
62:             transitions_path,
63:         };
64:         if predictor.learned.len() > MAX_LEARNED {
65:             let start = predictor.learned.len() - MAX_LEARNED;
66:             predictor.learned = predictor.learned.split_off(start);
67:         }
68:         if predictor.transitions.len() > MAX_TRANSITIONS {
69:             let start = predictor.transitions.len() - MAX_TRANSITIONS;
70:             predictor.transitions = predictor.transitions.split_off(start);
71:         }
72:         predictor
73:     }
74: 
75:     pub fn add_project_lines(&mut self, lines: Vec<String>) {
76:         if lines.is_empty() {
77:             return;
78:         }
79:         self.project.extend(
80:             lines
81:                 .into_iter()
82:                 .filter(|line| !line.is_empty() && line.chars().count() <= MAX_LINE),
83:         );
84:         if self.project.len() > MAX_PROJECT_LINES {
85:             let start = self.project.len() - MAX_PROJECT_LINES;
86:             self.project.drain(0..start);
87:         }
88:     }
89: 
90:     pub fn suggest(
91:         &self,
92:         corpus: &[&str],
93:         line: &str,
94:         col: usize,
95:         previous: Option<&str>,
96:         indent: &str,
97:     ) -> Option<String> {
98:         let prefix: String = line.chars().take(col).collect();
99:         if prefix.trim().is_empty() {
100:             return self.suggest_next(corpus, previous, indent);
101:         }
102:         self.suggest_line(corpus, &prefix, previous, indent)
103:     }
104: 
105:     fn suggest_line(
106:         &self,
107:         corpus: &[&str],
108:         prefix: &str,
109:         previous: Option<&str>,
110:         indent: &str,
111:     ) -> Option<String> {
112:         let keys = suffix_keys(prefix);
113:         if keys.is_empty() {
114:             return None;
115:         }
116: 
117:         for key in &keys {
118:             let mut scores: BTreeMap<String, (u32, u32)> = BTreeMap::new();
119: 
120:             for (index, candidate) in corpus.iter().enumerate() {
121:                 let previous_candidate = index
122:                     .checked_sub(1)
123:                     .and_then(|index| corpus.get(index))
124:                     .copied();
125:                 self.score_candidate(
126:                     candidate,
127:                     previous_candidate,
128:                     key,
129:                     previous,
130:                     indent,
131:                     1,
132:                     &mut scores,
133:                 );
134:             }
135:             for (index, candidate) in self.project.iter().enumerate() {
136:                 let previous_candidate = index
137:                     .checked_sub(1)
138:                     .and_then(|index| self.project.get(index))
139:                     .map(String::as_str);
140:                 self.score_candidate(
141:                     candidate,
142:                     previous_candidate,
143:                     key,
144:                     previous,
145:                     indent,
146:                     1,
147:                     &mut scores,
148:                 );
149:             }
150:             for candidate in &self.learned {
151:                 self.score_candidate(candidate, None, key, previous, indent, 4, &mut scores);
152:             }
153: 
154:             if let Some(best) = pick_best(scores) {
155:                 return Some(best);
156:             }
157:         }
158: 
159:         None
160:     }
161: 
162:     fn suggest_next(
163:         &self,
164:         corpus: &[&str],
165:         previous: Option<&str>,
166:         indent: &str,
167:     ) -> Option<String> {
168:         let previous = previous?;
169:         let key = tail_key(previous, MAX_KEY)?;
170:         let mut scores: BTreeMap<String, (u32, u32)> = BTreeMap::new();
171: 
172:         for (known, next) in &self.transitions {
173:             if known == &key {
174:                 add_next(&mut scores, next, indent, 4);
175:             } else if known.ends_with(&key) {
176:                 add_next(&mut scores, next, indent, 2);
177:             }
178:         }
179:         for pair in corpus.windows(2) {
180:             if tail_key(pair[0], MAX_KEY).as_deref() == Some(key.as_str()) {
181:                 add_next(&mut scores, pair[1], indent, 1);
182:             }
183:         }
184:         for pair in self.project.windows(2) {
185:             if tail_key(&pair[0], MAX_KEY).as_deref() == Some(key.as_str()) {
186:                 add_next(&mut scores, &pair[1], indent, 1);
187:             }
188:         }
189: 
190:         pick_best(scores)
191:     }
192: 
193:     #[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
194:     fn score_candidate(
195:         &self,
196:         candidate: &str,
197:         previous_candidate: Option<&str>,
198:         key: &str,
199:         previous: Option<&str>,
200:         indent: &str,
201:         weight: u32,
202:         scores: &mut BTreeMap<String, (u32, u32)>,
203:     ) {
204:         let Some(position) = candidate.rfind(key) else {
205:             return;
206:         };
207:         let tail = candidate[position + key.len()..].trim_end();
208:         if tail.is_empty() || tail.chars().count() > MAX_TAIL || tail.chars().any(char::is_control)
209:         {
210:             return;
211:         }
212: 
213:         let mut score = weight * 2 + key.chars().count() as u32;
214:         let candidate_indent = leading_whitespace(candidate);
215:         if candidate_indent == indent {
216:             score += 4;
217:         } else if !candidate_indent.is_empty() && candidate_indent.starts_with(indent) {
218:             score += 1;
219:         }
220:         if let (Some(previous), Some(previous_candidate)) = (previous, previous_candidate) {
221:             if let Some(previous_key) = tail_key(previous, 12) {
222:                 if previous_candidate.trim_end().ends_with(&previous_key) {
223:                     score += 4;
224:                 }
225:             }
226:         }
227: 
228:         scores
229:             .entry(tail.to_string())
230:             .and_modify(|(total, count)| {
231:                 *total += score;
232:                 *count += 1;
233:             })
234:             .or_insert((score, 1));
235:     }
236: 
237:     pub fn learn(&mut self, line: &str) {
238:         let line = line.trim_end();
239:         if line.trim().is_empty() || line.chars().count() > MAX_LINE {
240:             return;
241:         }
242: 
243:         self.learned.retain(|known| known != line);
244:         self.learned.push(line.to_string());
245:         if self.learned.len() > MAX_LEARNED {
246:             self.learned.remove(0);
247:         }
248: 
249:         let Some(path) = &self.path else {
250:             return;
251:         };
252:         if let Some(parent) = path.parent() {
253:             let _ = fs::create_dir_all(parent);
254:         }
255:         if let Ok(mut file) = OpenOptions::new().create(true).append(true).open(path) {
256:             let _ = writeln!(file, "{line}");
257:         }
258:     }
259: 
260:     pub fn learn_lines(&mut self, lines: &[String]) {
261:         for pair in lines.windows(2) {
262:             self.learn_transition(&pair[0], &pair[1]);
263:         }
264:     }
265: 
266:     pub fn learn_transition(&mut self, previous: &str, next: &str) {
267:         let Some(key) = tail_key(previous, MAX_KEY) else {
268:             return;
269:         };
270:         let next = next.trim_end();
271:         if next.trim().is_empty() || next.chars().count() > MAX_LINE {
272:             return;
273:         }
274:         if !next.starts_with(leading_whitespace(previous))
275:             && !leading_whitespace(previous).is_empty()
276:         {
277:             return;
278:         }
279: 
280:         if let Some(index) = self
281:             .transitions
282:             .iter()
283:             .position(|(known, line)| known == &key && line == next)
284:         {
285:             let entry = self.transitions.remove(index);
286:             self.transitions.push(entry);
287:         } else {
288:             self.transitions.push((key.clone(), next.to_string()));
289:             if self.transitions.len() > MAX_TRANSITIONS {
290:                 self.transitions.remove(0);
291:             }
292:         }
293: 
294:         let Some(path) = &self.transitions_path else {
295:             return;
296:         };
297:         if let Some(parent) = path.parent() {
298:             let _ = fs::create_dir_all(parent);
299:         }
300:         if let Ok(mut file) = OpenOptions::new().create(true).append(true).open(path) {
301:             let _ = writeln!(file, "{}", json!({ "p": key, "n": next }));
302:         }
303:     }
304: }
305: 
306: fn suffix_keys(prefix: &str) -> Vec<String> {
307:     let mut keys = Vec::new();
308:     for length in [16usize, 12, 8, 6, 4] {
309:         let key: String = prefix
310:             .chars()
311:             .rev()
312:             .take(length)
313:             .collect::<Vec<_>>()
314:             .into_iter()
315:             .rev()
316:             .collect();
317:         if key.trim().chars().count() < 3 || keys.contains(&key) {
318:             continue;
319:         }
320:         keys.push(key);
321:     }
322:     keys
323: }
324: 
325: fn tail_key(line: &str, length: usize) -> Option<String> {
326:     let line = line.trim_end();
327:     if line.chars().count() < 3 {
328:         return None;
329:     }
330:     let key: String = line
331:         .chars()
332:         .rev()
333:         .take(length)
334:         .collect::<Vec<_>>()
335:         .into_iter()
336:         .rev()
337:         .collect();
338:     (key.chars().count() >= 3).then_some(key)
339: }
340: 
341: fn add_next(scores: &mut BTreeMap<String, (u32, u32)>, next: &str, indent: &str, weight: u32) {
342:     let next = next.trim_end();
343:     if next.is_empty() || next.chars().count() > MAX_LINE || next.chars().any(char::is_control) {
344:         return;
345:     }
346:     let next_indent = leading_whitespace(next);
347:     if !next_indent.starts_with(indent) {
348:         return;
349:     }
350:     let tail: String = next.chars().skip(indent.chars().count()).collect();
351:     if tail.trim().is_empty() {
352:         return;
353:     }
354: 
355:     let mut score = weight * 3;
356:     if next_indent == indent {
357:         score += 4;
358:     } else if next_indent.starts_with(indent) {
359:         score += 2;
360:     }
361:     scores
362:         .entry(tail)
363:         .and_modify(|(total, count)| {
364:             *total += score;
365:             *count += 1;
366:         })
367:         .or_insert((score, 1));
368: }
369: 
370: fn pick_best(scores: BTreeMap<String, (u32, u32)>) -> Option<String> {
371:     let mut best: Option<(u32, u32, String)> = None;
372:     for (tail, (score, count)) in scores {
373:         if score < MIN_SCORE || (count < 2 && score < 12) {
374:             continue;
375:         }
376:         let replace = match &best {
377:             None => true,
378:             Some((best_score, best_count, best_tail)) => {
379:                 score > *best_score
380:                     || (score == *best_score && count > *best_count)
381:                     || (score == *best_score
382:                         && count == *best_count
383:                         && tail.len() < best_tail.len())
384:             }
385:         };
386:         if replace {
387:             best = Some((score, count, tail));
388:         }
389:     }
390:     best.map(|(_, _, tail)| tail)
391: }
392: 
393: fn leading_whitespace(line: &str) -> &str {
394:     let end = line
395:         .char_indices()
396:         .take_while(|(_, ch)| *ch == ' ' || *ch == '\t')
397:         .map(|(index, ch)| index + ch.len_utf8())
398:         .last()
399:         .unwrap_or(0);
400:     &line[..end]
401: }
402: 
403: fn cache_path(name: &str) -> Option<PathBuf> {
404:     if let Some(base) = env::var_os("XDG_CACHE_HOME") {
405:         return Some(PathBuf::from(base).join("smol").join(name));
406:     }
407:     env::var_os("HOME").map(|home| PathBuf::from(home).join(".cache").join("smol").join(name))
408: }
409: 
410: pub fn scan_project(root: &Path) -> Vec<String> {
411:     let mut files = Vec::new();
412:     collect_files(root, 0, &mut files);
413:     files.sort();
414: 
415:     let mut lines = Vec::new();
416:     let mut bytes = 0usize;
417:     for path in files.into_iter().take(300) {
418:         if bytes > 4 * 1024 * 1024 || lines.len() >= 20_000 {
419:             break;
420:         }
421:         let Ok(metadata) = fs::metadata(&path) else {
422:             continue;
423:         };
424:         if metadata.len() > 256 * 1024 {
425:             continue;
426:         }
427:         let Ok(data) = fs::read(&path) else {
428:             continue;
429:         };
430:         if data.contains(&0) {
431:             continue;
432:         }
433:         let Ok(text) = String::from_utf8(data) else {
434:             continue;
435:         };
436:         bytes += text.len();
437:         for line in text.split('\n') {
438:             let line = line.strip_suffix('\r').unwrap_or(line);
439:             if lines.len() >= 20_000 {
440:                 break;
441:             }
442:             if !line.is_empty() && line.chars().count() <= MAX_LINE {
443:                 lines.push(line.to_string());
444:             }
445:         }
446:     }
447:     lines
448: }
449: 
450: fn collect_files(root: &Path, depth: usize, files: &mut Vec<PathBuf>) {
451:     if depth > 8 {
452:         return;
453:     }
454:     let Ok(entries) = fs::read_dir(root) else {
455:         return;
456:     };
457:     for entry in entries.flatten() {
458:         let name = entry.file_name();
459:         let name = name.to_string_lossy();
460:         if IGNORED.contains(&name.as_ref()) {
461:             continue;
462:         }
463:         let path = entry.path();
464:         let Ok(file_type) = entry.file_type() else {
465:             continue;
466:         };
467:         if file_type.is_dir() {
468:             collect_files(&path, depth + 1, files);
469:         } else if file_type.is_file() {
470:             files.push(path);
471:         }
472:     }
473: }

230: #[derive(Clone)]
231: pub struct TextPrompt {
232:     pub title: String,
233:     pub value: String,
234:     pub cursor: usize,
235: }
236: 
237: impl TextPrompt {
238:     fn new(title: impl Into<String>, value: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
239:         let value = value.into();
240:         let cursor = value.chars().count();
241:         Self {
242:             title: title.into(),
243:             value,
244:             cursor,
245:         }
246:     }
247: 
248:     fn insert(&mut self, ch: char) {
249:         if ch.is_control() {
250:             return;
251:         }
252:         let mut chars: Vec<char> = self.value.chars().collect();
253:         chars.insert(self.cursor.min(chars.len()), ch);
254:         self.cursor = (self.cursor + 1).min(chars.len());
255:         self.value = chars.into_iter().collect();
256:     }
257: 
258:     fn insert_text(&mut self, text: &str) {
259:         for ch in text.chars().filter(|ch| !ch.is_control()) {
260:             self.insert(ch);
261:         }
262:     }
263: 
264:     fn backspace(&mut self) {
265:         if self.cursor == 0 {
266:             return;
267:         }
268:         let mut chars: Vec<char> = self.value.chars().collect();
269:         chars.remove(self.cursor - 1);
270:         self.cursor -= 1;
271:         self.value = chars.into_iter().collect();
272:     }
273: 
274:     fn delete(&mut self) {
275:         let mut chars: Vec<char> = self.value.chars().collect();
276:         if self.cursor < chars.len() {
277:             chars.remove(self.cursor);
278:             self.value = chars.into_iter().collect();
279:         }
280:     }
281: 
282:     fn left(&mut self) {
283:         self.cursor = self.cursor.saturating_sub(1);
284:     }
285: 
286:     fn right(&mut self) {
287:         self.cursor = (self.cursor + 1).min(self.value.chars().count());
288:     }
289: }
